\begin{tabular}{|c|c|}
\hline
\textbf{Operation} & \textbf{Complexity} \\
\hline

put ()
&
O ($\log$ n)
\\
\hline

get ()
&
O ($\log$ n)
\\
\hline

remove ()
&
O ($\log$ n)
\\
\hline

containsKey ()
&
O ($\log$ n)
\\
\hline

firstKey ()
&
O ($\log$ n)
\\
\hline

lastKey ()
&
O ($\log$ n)
\\
\hline

\end{tabular}

\end{center}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{HashMap vs TreeMap}

\begin{center}

\begin{tabular}{|c|c|c|}
\hline
\textbf{Property} &
\textbf{HashMap} &
\textbf{TreeMap} \\
\hline

Structure &
Hash Table &
Red-Black Tree \\
\hline

Ordering &
No &
Yes \\
\hline

Lookup &
O (1) &
O ($\log$ n) \\
\hline

Insertion &
O (1) &
O ($\log$ n) \\
\hline

Range Queries &
Poor &
Excellent \\
\hline

\end{tabular}

\end{center}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Deep Connection}

Observe:

HashSet $\blacktriangleright$ stores keys only.

HashMap $\blacktriangleright$ stores key-value pairs.

TreeSet $\blacktriangleright$ stores ordered keys.

TreeMap $\blacktriangleright$ stores ordered key-value pairs.

Everything is becoming symmetrical.
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Collection Family Tree}

\begin{verbatim}

Set             Map
 |               |   
 +-- HashSet     +-- HashMap
 |               |
 +-- TreeSet     +-- TreeMap

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The OOP View}

TreeMap depends on:
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\begin{itemize}
\item \textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Abstraction}
(Map)

\item \textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Polymorphism}

\item \textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Interfaces}

\item \textcolor{RosettaJade}{Comparable}

\item \textcolor{RosettaJade}{Comparator}
\end{itemize}
\emph{Many previous chapters} converge here.
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

TreeMap stores key-value pairs.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Keys remain sorted.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

TreeMap uses comparison.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

TreeMap uses a Red-Black Tree.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

TreeMap operations are O (log n).

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

TreeMap supports range queries.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Keys must be comparable.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Ordering comes from
Comparable or Comparator.

\end{rosettarule}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Mental Compression}
\begin{itemize}
\item HashMap $\blacktriangleright$
Key + Value + \textcolor{RosettaJade}{\textbf{Hashing}}.

\item TreeMap $\blacktriangleright$
Key + Value + \textcolor{RosettaOrange}{\textbf{Ordering}}.

\item HashMap \textbf{O (1)}

\item TreeMap \textbf{O ($\log$ n)}

\item HashMap \textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{Fast}}

\item TreeMap \textcolor{RosettaOrange}{\textbf{Sorted}}
\end{itemize}
\chapter[Queue and PriorityQueue]{Queue and PriorityQueue --- The Collection of Importance}

\section{The Thirty Fourth Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

\emph{Many beginners think}:

\textcolor{RosettaJade}{Queue} = \textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{ArrayList}
with restrictions.

\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{False}.

Queue represents a completely
different philosophy.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Lists focus on \emph{storage}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Queues focus on \emph{processing order}.
\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{The Real World Inspiration}

% Imagine:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% People waiting at a ticket counter.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% First person arrives.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% First person served.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% This is:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% FIFO

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% First In First Out

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Real World Inspiration}

\textbf{Imagine:}

\begin{quote}
People waiting at a ticket counter.
\end{quote}

The first person arrives.

The first person served.

\smallskip

\begin{center}
{\Large\bfseries\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{FIFO}}

\smallskip

\textit{First In First Out}
\end{center}
\section{Queue Interface}

Java provides:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Queue<E>
\end{minted}
\newpage
Conceptually:

\begin{verbatim}

Front
  |
  v

[A][B][C][D]

           ^
           |
          Rear

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Insert at rear.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Remove from front.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Basic Operations}

% \begin{minted}{java} 
% offer()
% poll()
% peek()
% \end{minted}

% Think:

% offer () = enqueue

% poll () = dequeue

% peek () = look at front
% without removing.

\section{Basic Operations}

\begin{minted}{java}
offer();
poll();
peek();
\end{minted}

\medskip

\textbf{Think of them as:}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcl@{}}
\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\texttt{offer()}} & $\rightarrow$ & enqueue \\

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\texttt{poll()}} & $\rightarrow$ & dequeue \\

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\texttt{peek()}} & $\rightarrow$ & look at the front without removing
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Example}

\begin{minted}{java} 
Queue<Integer> q = new LinkedList<>();
q.offer(10);
q.offer(20);
q.offer(30);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Queue becomes:

\begin{verbatim}

Front

    10 20 30

            Rear

\end{verbatim}
\newpage
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Polling}
Observe:
\begin{minted}{java} 
q.poll();
\end{minted}

Removes:

\[
10
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Queue becomes:

\begin{minted}{java}
    20 30
\end{minted}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{FIFO} behavior.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Peek}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
q.peek();
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Returns:

\[
20
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Does not remove it.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Why LinkedList Works Well}

% Queue requires:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Insert at rear.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Remove at front.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Linked lists naturally support this.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Applications}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Printer jobs.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Customer service systems.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Breadth First Search.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Operating system scheduling.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Network packet processing.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%


\section{Why LinkedList Works Well}

Queue requires:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
    \item Insert at rear.
    \item Remove at front.
\end{itemize}

\medskip

\textbf{Observation:}

\begin{quote}
Linked lists naturally support this.
\end{quote}
\section{Applications}

Queues appear naturally in many real-world systems.

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
    \item Printer jobs
    \item Customer service systems
    \item Breadth First Search (BFS)
    \item Operating system scheduling
    \item Network packet processing
\end{itemize}

% \section{The Limitation}

% Suppose:

% \begin{verbatim}

% Task A
% Priority 1

% Task B
% Priority 100

% Task C
% Priority 5

% \end{verbatim}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% FIFO says:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Process oldest first.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% But maybe:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Highest priority should go first.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Queue cannot do this.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Limitation}

\textbf{Suppose}

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{l}
\texttt{Task A \qquad Priority 1}\\[2mm]
\texttt{Task B \qquad Priority 100}\\[2mm]
\texttt{Task C \qquad Priority 5}
\end{array}
}
\]

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{FIFO says}}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item Process oldest first.
\end{itemize}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaJade}{\textbf{But maybe}}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item Highest priority should go first.
\end{itemize}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaRed}{\textbf{Observation}}

Queue cannot do this.

\section{The New Question}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{Not}}:

    --- Who arrived first?

\item \textbf{\textcolor{RosettaSeaGreen}{Instead}}:

    --- Who is most important?

\item This creates:

    --- {\ttfamily\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{PriorityQueue}}
\end{itemize}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{PriorityQueue Creation}

\begin{minted}{java} 
PriorityQueue<Integer> pq = new PriorityQueue<>();
\end{minted}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
Insert:

\begin{minted}{java} 
pq.offer(50);
pq.offer(10);
pq.offer(30);
pq.offer(20);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\question{What is at the front}

Answer: {\ttfamily\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{10}}}

\emph{Smallest} element.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{The Surprise}

% Insertion order:

% \begin{verbatim}

% 50
% 10
% 30
% 20

% \end{verbatim}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Removal order:

% \begin{verbatim}

% 10
% 20
% 30
% 50

% \end{verbatim}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Priority determines processing.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Not arrival time.

\section{The Surprise}

\begin{center}
\begin{minipage}{0.42\textwidth}
\centering
\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Insertion Order}}

\vspace{0.5em}

\fcolorbox{RosettaGray}{RosettaGrayBg}{
\begin{minipage}{0.18\textwidth}
\centering
\ttfamily
50

10

30

20
\end{minipage}
}

\end{minipage}
\hfill
{\Large\textcolor{RosettaGray}{$\Longrightarrow$}}
\hfill
\begin{minipage}{0.42\textwidth}
\centering
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Removal Order}}

\vspace{0.5em}

\fcolorbox{RosettaGray}{RosettaGrayBg}{
\begin{minipage}{0.18\textwidth}
\centering
\ttfamily
10

20

30

50
\end{minipage}
}

\end{minipage}
\end{center}

\bigskip

\noindent
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{Key Insight}}

\begin{quote}
Priority determines processing.

\medskip

\textbf{Not arrival time.}
\end{quote}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{The Hidden Structure}

% PriorityQueue is usually implemented using:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Binary Heap

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Not a tree set.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Not a hash table.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% A heap.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Heap Mental Model}

% Min Heap:

% \begin{verbatim}

%        10
%       /  \
%     20    30
%    / \
%  50  40

% \end{verbatim}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Rule:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Parent <= Children

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Smallest element always at root.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Why Heap Matters}

% \textbf{\textbf{Question:}}

% How quickly can we find
% smallest element?

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Answer:

% \[
% O(1)
% \]

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Look at root.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Insertion Complexity}

% Insert:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Place at bottom.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Bubble upward.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Cost:

% \[
% O(\log n)
% \]

\section{The Hidden Structure}

PriorityQueue is usually implemented using

\begin{center}
{\Large\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Binary Heap}}}
\end{center}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaRed}{\textbf{Not}}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
    \item a tree set
    \item a hash table
\end{itemize}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Implementation}}

A heap.
\section{Heap Mental Model}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Min Heap}}

\begin{center}
\begin{verbatim}
       10
      /  \
    20    30
   / \
 50  40
\end{verbatim}
\end{center}

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{Rule}}

\[
\boxed{\text{Parent} \le \text{Children}}
\]

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Observation}}

Smallest element always at root.
\section{Why Heap Matters}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Question}}

How quickly can we find the smallest element?

\bigskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Answer}}

\[
\boxed{O(1)}
\]

\begin{center}
Look at the root.
\end{center}
\section{Insertion Complexity}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Insertion}}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
    \item Place at the bottom.
    \item Bubble upward.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Time Complexity}}

\[
\boxed{O(\log n)}
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Removal Complexity}

% Remove root.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Move last element upward.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Heapify.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Cost:

% \[
% O(\log n)
% \]

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{PriorityQueue And Comparable}

% Observe:

% \begin{minted}{java} 
% PriorityQueue<Student>pq = new PriorityQueue<>();
% \end{minted}

% \question{How should students be ordered}

% Need:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Comparable

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% or

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Comparator

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Exactly like TreeSet.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Custom Comparator}

% \begin{minted}{java} 
% PriorityQueue<Student>
% pq =
% new PriorityQueue<>(
% new MarksComparator()
% );
% \end{minted}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Now highest marks may
% appear first.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{PriorityQueue vs TreeSet}

% Important.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% TreeSet:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Entire collection sorted.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% PriorityQueue:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Only highest-priority element
% guaranteed at top.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% The remaining elements need not
% appear sorted internally.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Complexity Table}

% \begin{center}

% \begin{tabular}{|c|c|}
% \hline
% Operation & Complexity \\
% \hline

% peek ()
% &
% O(1)
% \\
% \hline

% offer ()
% &
% O($\log $n)
% \\
% \hline

% poll ()
% &
% O($\log $n)
% \\
% \hline

% contains ()
% &
% O (n)
% \\
% \hline

% \end{tabular}

% \end{center}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Queue Family Tree}

% \begin{verbatim}

% Collection
%     |
%     Queue
%       |
% ---------------------
% |                   |
% LinkedList    PriorityQueue

% \end{verbatim}

\section{Removal Complexity}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Removal Steps}}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
    \item Remove the root.
    \item Move the last element upward.
    \item Heapify.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Time Complexity}}

\[
\boxed{O(\log n)}
\]
\section{PriorityQueue And Comparable}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Observe}}

\begin{minted}{java}
PriorityQueue<Student> pq = new PriorityQueue<>();
\end{minted}
\question{How should students be ordered}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaJade}{\textbf{Need}}

\begin{center}
\Large
\textbf{Comparable}

\vspace{0.4em}

\textcolor{RosettaGray}{or}

\vspace{0.4em}

\textbf{Comparator}
\end{center}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{Exactly like TreeSet.}}
\section{Custom Comparator}

\begin{minted}{java}
PriorityQueue<Student> pq = new PriorityQueue<>(new MarksComparator());
\end{minted}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Result}}

Highest marks may now appear first.
\section{PriorityQueue vs TreeSet}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.5}
\begin{tabular}{|p{0.28\linewidth}|p{0.58\linewidth}|}
\hline

\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{TreeSet}}
&
Entire collection remains sorted.
\\
\hline

\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaJade}{PriorityQueue}}
&
Only the highest-priority element is guaranteed at the top.
\\

&
The remaining elements need not appear sorted internally.
\\
\hline

\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\section{Complexity Table}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{|l|c|}
\hline
% \rowcolor{RosettaBlueBg}
\textbf{Operation} & \textbf{Complexity} \\
\hline
\texttt{peek ()}     & $\mathbf{O(1)}$ \\
\hline
\texttt{offer ()}   & $\mathbf{O(\log n)}$ \\
\hline
\texttt{poll ()}   & $\mathbf{O(\log n)}$ \\
\hline
\texttt{contains ()} & $\mathbf{O(n)}$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\section{Queue Family Tree}

\begin{center}
\setlength{\fboxsep}{8pt}
\begin{lrbox}{\myDiagramBox}
\begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
\begin{Verbatim}
    Collection
        |
        Queue
    ____|________________
    |                   |
LinkedList    PriorityQueue
\end{Verbatim}
\end{minipage}
\end{lrbox}
\fcolorbox{RosettaGray}{RosettaGrayBg}{\usebox{\myDiagramBox}}
\end{center}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

Queue follows FIFO.\@

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

offer () inserts.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

poll () removes front.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

peek () views front.
\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

PriorityQueue processes
most important element first.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

PriorityQueue uses a heap.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

PriorityQueue relies on
comparison logic.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

PriorityQueue is not fully sorted.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
% \section{Mental Compression}

% Queue

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% FIFO

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% PriorityQueue

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Importance First

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% LinkedList

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Queue Implementation

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Heap

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% PriorityQueue Implementation

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Comparable

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Ordering

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Comparator

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Custom Priority

\section{Mental Compression}
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ]\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Queue}}
\hspace{1em}
$\longmapsto$
\hspace{1em}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{FIFO}

\vspace{1.0em}

\item [ ]\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{PriorityQueue}}
\hspace{1em}
$\longmapsto$
\hspace{1em}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Importance First}

\vspace{1.0em}

\item [ ]\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{LinkedList}}
\hspace{1em}
$\longmapsto$
\hspace{1em}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Queue Implementation}

\vspace{1.0em}

\item [ ]\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Heap}}
\hspace{1em}
$\longmapsto$
\hspace{1em}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{PriorityQueue Implementation}

\vspace{1.0em}

\item [ ]\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Comparable}}
\hspace{1em}
$\longmapsto$
\hspace{1em}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Ordering}

\vspace{1.0em}

\item [ ]\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Comparator}}
\hspace{1em}
$\longmapsto$
\hspace{1em}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Custom Priority}
\end{itemize}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{Iterator --- The Universal Collection Navigator}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\smallskip
\noindent
\hyperref[sec:quick_nav]{\emph{Back to Quick Navigation}}

\smallskip
\noindent
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{$\Rightarrow$}
\emph{Fundamental to \hyperref[chap:collections]{Collections Framework}. Part of the \hyperref[chap:interfaces]{Interfaces} design pattern.}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Thirty Fifth Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

\emph{Many beginners think}:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
for-loop = the way to traverse collections.
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{False}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

for-loop only works naturally
for indexed structures.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

HashSet has no index.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

TreeSet has no index.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Queue has no index.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Something more general is needed.

\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Fundamental Problem}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList<Integer> list;
\end{minted}

Traversal:

\begin{minted}{java} 
for(int i=0;i<list.size();i++) {
    System.out.println(list.get(i));
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Works.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Now:

\begin{minted}{java} 
HashSet<Integer> set;
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\question{What is}
\begin{minted}{java}
set.get (i)
\end{minted}


%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Doesn't exist.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

No indexing.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The OOP Requirement}

Java wants:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

One traversal mechanism
for all collections.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

That mechanism is:

\[
\textbf{Iterator}
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Core Idea}

Think:
 Iterator = Collection Cursor

A movable pointer.
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Mental Picture}

Suppose:

% \begin{verbatim}

% 10   20   30   40

% ^

% Iterator

% \end{verbatim}
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[
node/.style={
draw,
rounded corners=2pt,
minimum width=0.9cm,
minimum height=0.65cm,
fill=RosettaBlueBg,
draw=RosettaBlue
}
]

\node[node] (a) at (0,0) {10};
\node[node] (b) at (1.5,0) {20};
\node[node] (c) at (3,0) {30};
\node[node] (d) at (4.5,0) {40};

\node[
text=RosettaRoyalPurple,
font=\Large
] at (0,-0.35) {$\blacktriangle$};

\node[
text=RosettaRoyalPurple,
font=\small\bfseries
] at (0,-0.95) {Iterator};
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

The iterator points at the
(\emph{next}) element to visit.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Interface}

Conceptually:

\begin{minted}{java} 
interface Iterator<E> {
    boolean hasNext();
    E next();
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Two methods.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Huge power.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{hasNext ()}
% \question{Is there another element}
% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Returns:

% \begin{minted}{java} 
% true
% \end{minted}

% or

% \begin{minted}{java} 
% false
% \end{minted}
\section{hasNext ()}

\question{Is there another element}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Returns}}

\begin{center}

\begin{minted}{java}
true
\end{minted}

\textcolor{RosettaGray}{\textbf{or}}

\begin{minted}{java}
false
\end{minted}

\end{center}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{next ()}

Returns:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Current element.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\emph{Then advances \textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{cursor}}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% \section{Visualization}

% Initial:

% \begin{verbatim}

% 10 20 30 40

% ^

% \end{verbatim}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% After:

% \begin{minted}{java} 
% next()
% \end{minted}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Returns:

% \[
% 10
% \]

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Cursor moves:

% \begin{verbatim}

% 10 20 30 40

%     ^

% \end{verbatim}

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Visualization}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Initial State}}

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[
cell/.style={
draw=RosettaBlue,
fill=RosettaBlueBg,
minimum width=1cm,
minimum height=0.8cm,
font=\ttfamily
}
]

\node[cell] (a) at (0,0) {10};
\node[cell] (b) at (1.4,0) {20};
\node[cell] (c) at (2.8,0) {30};
\node[cell] (d) at (4.2,0) {40};

\node[text=RosettaRoyalPurple,font=\Large] at (0,-0.45) {$\blacktriangle$};

\node[
text=RosettaRoyalPurple,
font=\small\bfseries
] at (0,-1.0) {Iterator};

\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaJade}{\textbf{Call}}

\begin{minted}{java}
next()
\end{minted}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Returns}}

\[
\boxed{10}
\]

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{Cursor Moves}}

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[
cell/.style={
draw=RosettaBlue,
fill=RosettaBlueBg,
minimum width=1cm,
minimum height=0.8cm,
font=\ttfamily
}
]

\node[cell] (a) at (0,0) {10};
\node[cell] (b) at (1.4,0) {20};
\node[cell] (c) at (2.8,0) {30};
\node[cell] (d) at (4.2,0) {40};

\node[text=RosettaRoyalPurple,font=\Large] at (1.4,-0.45) {$\blacktriangle$};

\node[
text=RosettaRoyalPurple,
font=\small\bfseries
] at (1.4,-1.0) {Iterator};

\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}

\section{Obtaining Iterator}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Iterator<Integer> it = list.iterator();
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Every collection can create
an appropriate iterator.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Canonical Traversal}

\begin{minted}{java} 
Iterator<Integer> it = list.iterator();
while(it.hasNext()) {
    System.out.println(it.next());
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Works for:
\begin{itemize}
\item ArrayList
\item LinkedList
\item HashSet
\item TreeSet
\item PriorityQueue
\item Many others.
\end{itemize}

\section{The Deep OOP Insight}

Observe:

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{ArrayList}
stores data differently from
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{HashSet}

Yet both provide:

\begin{minted}{java} 
iterator()
\end{minted}

\textcolor{olive}{Polymorphism} again.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Iterator Hides Structure}

User sees:

\begin{minted}{java} 
hasNext()
next()
\end{minted}

User does not see:
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ]Arrays
\item [ ]Linked Lists
\item [ ]Trees
\item [ ]Hash Tables
\end{itemize}

Classic \textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Abstraction}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Hidden Relationship}

Observe:

\begin{verbatim}

Iterable
    |
Collection
    |
ArrayList

\end{verbatim}

Important:

Collections implement

\[
Iterable
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Iterable}

Conceptually:

\begin{minted}{java} 
interface Iterable<E> {
    Iterator<E> iterator();
}
\end{minted}

Meaning:

`I can produce an iterator.'

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Enhanced For Loop Mystery}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
for(Integer x : list) {
    System.out.println(x);
}
\end{minted}

Looks magical.

Actually becomes approximately:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Iterator<Integer> it = list.iterator();
while(it.hasNext()) {
    Integer x = it.next();
    ...
}
\end{minted}


The enhanced loop is \textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\emph{syntax sugar}}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Removing Elements}

Iterator also provides:

\begin{minted}{java} 
remove()
\end{minted}

for safe deletion during traversal.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Dangerous Code}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
for(Integer x : list) {
    list.remove(x);
}
\end{minted}

Often causes:

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{ConcurrentModificationException}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Safe Removal}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Iterator<Integer> it = list.iterator();
while(it.hasNext()) {
    Integer x = it.next();
    if(x % 2 == 0) {
        it.remove();
    }
}
\end{minted}

\textcolor{RosettaSeaGreen}{Safe}.

Iterator understands traversal state.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Iterator And Maps}

Map is special.

Usually traverse:

\begin{minted}{java} 
map.entrySet()
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

then obtain iterator.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
% \section{Mental Model}

% Think:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Collection

% =

% Book

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Iterator

% =

% Bookmark

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Bookmark remembers:

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Current position.

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Mental Model}

\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{\textbf{Think}}

\medskip

\begin{center}

\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.6}

\begin{tabular}{p{0.32\linewidth} c p{0.42\linewidth}}

\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Collection}}
&
$\Longleftrightarrow$
&
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Book}

\\[0.5em]

\textbf{\textcolor{RosettaBlue}{Iterator}}
&
$\Longleftrightarrow$
&
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Bookmark}

\end{tabular}

\end{center}

\medskip

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{The Bookmark Remembers}}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
    \item Current position.
\end{itemize}

\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

Iterator provides universal traversal.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

hasNext () checks for remaining elements.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

next () returns current element.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Iterator hides internal structure.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Enhanced for-loop uses Iterator internally.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Iterable produces Iterators.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Iterator is an abstraction.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Safe removal should use iterator.remove ().

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
% \section{Mental Compression}

% Collection

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Stores data

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Iterator

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Traverses data

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Iterable

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Creates iterator

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% hasNext()

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% More elements?

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% next()

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Give next element

% %%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

% Enhanced For Loop

% \[
% \Rightarrow
% \]

% Iterator Sugar
\section{Mental Compression}

\begin{center}

\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.7}

\begin{tabular}{p{0.33\linewidth}c p{0.45\linewidth}}

\textbf{Collection}
&
$\rightarrow$
&
Stores data

\\

\textbf{Iterator}
&
$\rightarrow$
&
Traverses data

\\

\textbf{Iterable}
&
$\rightarrow$
&
Creates an iterator

\\

\texttt{hasNext ()}
&
$\rightarrow$
&
Checks for another element

\\

\texttt{next ()}
&
$\rightarrow$
&
Returns the next element

\\

Enhanced \texttt{for}
&
$\rightarrow$
&
Iterator syntax sugar

\end{tabular}

\end{center}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{Generics --- Teaching Classes About Types}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Thirty Sixth Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

\emph{Many beginners think}:

Generics
=
Angle Brackets.

\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{False}.

Generics are Java's way of
writing algorithms and data
structures independent of type.

The angle brackets are merely
the syntax.

\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Historical Problem}

Before Java 5:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList list = new ArrayList();
\end{minted}

Everything became:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Object
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Example}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList list = new ArrayList();
list.add("Alice");
list.add(42);
\end{minted}

Perfectly legal.

\question{What does list contain}
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ]Strings.
\item [ ]Integers.
\item [ ]Anything.
\end{itemize}
Compiler cannot help.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Disaster}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
String name = (String)list.get(1);
\end{minted}

Runtime:

\begin{minted}{java}
ClassCastException
\end{minted}

Program compiles.

Fails later.
\textcolor{RosettaRed}{Bad design}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Goal}

Instead of:

Checking types at runtime.

Check types during compilation.

This creates:
\textcolor{RosettaJade}{Generics}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The First Generic Example}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList<String> names = new ArrayList<>();
\end{minted}

Meaning:
This list stores Strings.

\emph{Nothing else}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Compiler Protection}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
names.add("Alice");
\end{minted}

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Valid}.

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
names.add(42);
\end{minted}

\textcolor{RosettaRed}{Compiler Error}.

Problem caught early.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Deep Insight}
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ]Without Generics:
Programmer promises type safety.
\item [ ]With Generics:
Compiler enforces type safety.
\end{itemize}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Creating Our Own Generic Class}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class Box<T> {
    private T value;
    public void set(T value){
        this.value = value;
    }
    public T get(){
        return value;
    }
}
\end{minted}

\question{What is T}
A type parameter.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Mental Model}

Think:

T = Placeholder Like:

x in mathematics.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Using Box}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Box<Integer> b = new Box<>();
\end{minted}

Compiler mentally thinks:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class Box {
    Integer value;
}
\end{minted}
Conceptually.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Another Example}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Box<String> b = new Box<>();
\end{minted}

Now:

\begin{minted}{java} 
String value;
\end{minted}

Same class.

Different type.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Why This Is Powerful}

\begin{itemize}
\item[ ]Without Generics:
    \begin{itemize}
    \item[ ]BoxForInteger
    \item[ ]BoxForString
    \item[ ]BoxForDouble
    \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}
Many classes.
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ]With Generics:
    \begin{itemize}
    \item[ ]One class.
    \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}
Many types.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Multiple Type Parameters}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class Pair<K,V> {
    K key;
    V value;
}
\end{minted}

Exactly the idea used by:
\textcolor{RosettaJade}{Map}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Example}

\begin{minted}{java} 
Pair<String,Integer> student;
\end{minted}

Meaning:

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{String} \textcolor{RosettaOrange}{key}

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Integer} \textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{value}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Generic Methods}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
public static <T> void print(T value) {
    System.out.println(value);
}
\end{minted}

Method itself becomes generic.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Invocation}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
print("Hello");
print(42);
print(3.14);
\end{minted}

Same method.

Different types.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Collection Revolution}

Generics transformed collections.

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<String>
\end{minted}

Compiler knows: Elements are Strings.

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Iterator<String>
\end{minted}

Compiler knows: next () returns String.

No cast required.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Bounded Generics}

Sometimes,any type is too broad.

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class Box<T extends Number> {
}
\end{minted}

Allowed:
\begin{itemize}
\item [-] \textcolor{RosettaJade}{Integer}
\item [-] \textcolor{RosettaJade}{Double}
\item [-] \textcolor{RosettaJade}{Float}
\end{itemize}
\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{Not} \textcolor{RosettaJade}{String}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Meaning Of extends}

Important.

Here:

\begin{minted}{java} 
T extends Number
\end{minted}

means:

T must be \textcolor{RosettaJade}{Number}
or a \emph{subclass}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Big Surprise}

Generics exist only during compilation.

This idea is called:
\[\ttfamily{\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Type Erasure}}\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Type Erasure}

Compiler checks \emph{types}

Then removes most {\ttfamily{generic}}
information.

At {\ttfamily{\textcolor{RosettaPurple}{\textbf{runtime}}}}:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList<String>
\end{minted}

and

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList<Integer>
\end{minted}

become roughly:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Why Type Erasure Exists}

Historical reason.

Backward compatibility.

Old Java code continues working.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Limitation}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
new T();
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Illegal.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Because \textcolor{RosettaPurple}{runtime}: no longer knows
what \textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{T}} really is.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Generics And OOP}
\begin{itemize}
\item []\textcolor{RosettaJade}{Generics} add:

    --- Type Abstraction

\item []\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{OOP}} already provided:

    --- Behavior Abstraction
\end{itemize}

    Together they create reusable APIs.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generics provide compile-time type safety.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

T is a type parameter.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generic classes work with many types.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generic methods are possible.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Bounds use extends.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generics reduce casting.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generics improve API design.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Java implements generics using type erasure.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Mental Compression}

\begin{center}

\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.55}

\begin{tabular}{p{0.30\linewidth}
                c
                p{0.48\linewidth}}

\textbf{Object}
&
$\Rightarrow$
&
Any Type
\\

\textbf{Generic}
&
$\Rightarrow$
&
Specific Type
\\

\texttt{T}
&
$\Rightarrow$
&
Type Variable
\\

\textbf{Compiler}
&
$\Rightarrow$
&
Type Checking
\\

\textbf{Type Erasure}
&
$\Rightarrow$
&
Runtime Simplification
\\

\textbf{Generics}
&
$\Rightarrow$
&
Reusable + Safe

\end{tabular}

\end{center}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{Wildcards --- ?, extends, super and PECS}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\smallskip
\noindent
\hyperref[sec:quick_nav]{\emph{Back to Quick Navigation}}

\smallskip
\noindent
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{$\Rightarrow$}
\emph{Advanced \hyperref[chap:generics]{Generics}. Depends on \hyperref[chap:inheritance]{Inheritance} and type relationships.}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Thirty Seventh Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

\emph{Many beginners think:}

\ttfamily{\textcolor{RosettaJade}{List}<\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Integer}> is a subtype of \textcolor{RosettaJade}{List}<\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Number}>}

\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{False}

This is one of the most
important surprises in Java.

\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Natural Expectation}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Integer extends Number
\end{minted}

Therefore many people expect:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer>
\end{minted}

to be a subtype of:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Number>
\end{minted}

But Java says: 
\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{No}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\newpage
\section{Why Java Refuses}

Suppose Java allowed:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer> ints = new ArrayList<>();
List<Number> nums = ints;
\end{minted}

Then:

\begin{minted}{java} 
nums.add(3.14);
\end{minted}

\question{Where does the Double go}

Into:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer>
\end{minted}

\textbf{Disaster}.
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Type safety} destroyed.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Core Rule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generics are invariant.

\end{rosettarule}

Meaning:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer>
\end{minted}

is NOT a subtype of:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Number>
\end{minted}

Even though:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Integer extends Number
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Question Wildcards Solve}

Suppose we want:

A method that accepts
\begin{minted}{java}
List<Integer>
List<Double>
List<Float>
List<Long>
\end{minted}

How?
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{\textbf{Wildcards}}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Simplest Wildcard}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<?>
\end{minted}

Read as:
List of something of
Unknown type.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Example}

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer> a;
List<String> b;
List<Double> c;
\end{minted}

All may be assigned to:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<?>
\end{minted}

Because:
We don't care about the exact type.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Limitation}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<?> list;
\end{minted}

Can we do:

\begin{minted}{java} 
list.add(10);
\end{minted}

?

\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{No}.

Compiler Error.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Why?}

Because:

What if actual type is:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<String>
\end{minted}

?


Adding Integer would be unsafe.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Read-Only View}

Think:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<?>
\end{minted}

as:

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Safe} for reading.

\textcolor{RosettaRed}{Unsafe} for writing.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Upper Bounded Wildcards}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<? extends Number>
\end{minted}

Read as: 
List of some type
that extends Number.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{What Can Match?}

Valid:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer>
List<Double>
List<Float>
List<Long>
List<Number>
\end{minted}

All accepted.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Big Benefit}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
double sum(List<? extends Number>list)
\end{minted}

Now method works for:
\begin{itemize}
\item [ > ]Integers
\item [ > ]Doubles
\item [ > ]Floats
\item [ > ]Anything numeric.
\end{itemize}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Reading Is Safe}

\question{Can we read}

\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Yes}.

Every element is at least:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Number
\end{minted}

Therefore:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Number n = list.get(0);
\end{minted}

Safe.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Writing Is Dangerous}

Can we do:

\begin{minted}{java} 
list.add(10);
\end{minted}

?

\textcolor{RosettaRed}{No}.

\question{What if actual type is}:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Double>
\end{minted}

Integer insertion breaks safety.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Producer Idea}

Think:

The list produces values.

We read from it.

Hence:

\begin{rosettarule}

Producer Extends.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Lower Bounded Wildcards}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<? super Integer>
\end{minted}

Read as:
List of Integer
or any \textcolor{blue}{superclass}.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Possible Matches}

Valid:

\begin{minted}{java} 
List<Integer>
List<Number>
List<Object>
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Big Difference}

Now:

\begin{minted}{java} 
list.add(10);
\end{minted}

\textcolor{RosettaSeaGreen}{Safe}.

Because every valid target
can store Integer.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Reading Is Hard}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Object x = list.get(0);
\end{minted}

Only Object guaranteed.

Compiler cannot promise more.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Consumer Idea}

Think:
List consumes Integers.

We insert into it.

Hence:

\begin{rosettarule}

Consumer Super.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{PECS}

The most important wildcard rule.

\textbf{PECS} : 
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ] \textbf{P}roducer
\item [ ] \textbf{E}xtends
\item [ ] \textbf{C}onsumer
\item [ ] \textbf{S}uper
\end{itemize}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Visualization}

\begin{verbatim}

    --- Read From
    ---     |
    ---     v
    --- 
    --- ? extends Number
    --- 
    --- Producer
    --- 
    --- --------------------
    --- 
    --- Write To
    ---     |
    ---     v
    --- 
    --- ? super Integer
    --- 
    --- Consumer

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Framework Connection}

Many professional APIs use:
\begin{itemize}
\item [ ] \textcolor{RosettaRed}{?} \textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{extends}
\item [ ] \textcolor{RosettaRed}{?} \textcolor{blue}{super}
\end{itemize}
everywhere.

\begin{itemize}
\item [-]Streams
\item [-]Collections
\item [-]Frameworks
\item [-]Libraries
\end{itemize}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Common Interview Question}

\textbf{Why can't:}

\begin{minted}{java}
List<Integer>
\end{minted}

\textbf{be assigned to:}

\begin{minted}{java}
List<Number>
\end{minted}

\textbf{?}

\textbf{Answer:}

Java generics are \textbf{invariant}.

Even though:
\[
Integer \subseteq Number
\]

it does \textbf{not} follow that:
\[
List<Integer> \subseteq List<Number>
\]

In other words, Java does not allow:
\begin{minted}{java}
List<Integer> li = new ArrayList<>();
List<Number> ln = li;    // ERROR
\end{minted}

\textbf{Why? Type safety.}

Suppose Java allowed this assignment:

\begin{minted}{java}
List<Integer> li = new ArrayList<>();
List<Number> ln = li;    // Suppose this were allowed

ln.add(3.14);            // Double is a Number
\end{minted}

Now the same underlying list, which is supposed to contain only
\texttt{Integer}s, contains a \texttt{Double}.

Later:

\begin{minted}{java}
Integer x = li.get(0);
\end{minted}

could fail because the object retrieved from the list may actually be a
\texttt{Double}.

Therefore Java prevents the assignment in the first place.

\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Integer is a Number:}
    \texttt{Integer} is a subtype of \texttt{Number}.

    \item \textbf{But List<Integer> is not List<Number>:}
    Generic types are invariant in Java.

    \item \textbf{Reason:}
    A \texttt{List<Number>} allows operations such as
    \texttt{add(Double)}, while a \texttt{List<Integer>} must reject them.

    \item \textbf{Conclusion:}
    Allowing the assignment would break the type-safety guarantee of
    \texttt{List<Integer>}.
\end{itemize}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

Generics are invariant.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

? means unknown type.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

? extends T means
some subtype of T.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

? super T means
some supertype of T.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Read from extends.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

Write into super.

\end{rosettarule}

\begin{rosettarule}

PECS:
Producer Extends
Consumer Super.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Mental Compression}

\subsection*{1. Invariance}

\[
Integer \subseteq Number
\]

but:

\[
List<Integer> \not\subseteq List<Number>
\]

Therefore:

\begin{minted}{java}
List<Integer> \neq List<Number>
\end{minted}

\textbf{Reason:} Generics are invariant in Java.

If \texttt{List<Integer>} were a \texttt{List<Number>}, we could add
a \texttt{Double} or \texttt{Float} to a list that must contain only
\texttt{Integer}s.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\subsection*{2. \texttt{? extends T}}

\[
? \text{ extends } T
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\textbf{Read safely as } T
\]

Think:

\[
\boxed{\text{Some unknown subtype of }T}
\]

Therefore:

\begin{minted}{java}
List<? extends Number> list;

Number x = list.get(0);    // SAFE
\end{minted}

But we cannot add a specific value:

\begin{minted}{java}
list.add(10);              // ERROR
\end{minted}

because we do not know what the actual element type is.

\textbf{Mental model:}

\[
\boxed{\texttt{extends} \Rightarrow \text{Producer}}
\]

It \textbf{produces} values for us to read.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\subsection*{3. \texttt{? super T}}

\[
? \text{ super } T
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\textbf{Write }T\textbf{ safely}
\]

Think:

\[
\boxed{\text{Some unknown supertype of }T}
\]

For example:

\begin{minted}{java}
List<? super Integer> list;

list.add(10);              // SAFE
list.add(20);              // SAFE
\end{minted}

Why?

Because whatever the actual type is, it is guaranteed to be able
to hold an \texttt{Integer}.

But when reading, we cannot assume the exact type:

\begin{minted}{java}
Object x = list.get(0);    // SAFE
\end{minted}

\textbf{Mental model:}

\[
\boxed{\texttt{super} \Rightarrow \text{Consumer}}
\]

It \textbf{consumes} values that we give it.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\subsection*{4. PECS}

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{P}roducer\;
\textbf{E}xtends
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{C}onsumer\;
\textbf{S}uper
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{\textbf{PECS}}
\]

\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{Wildcard} & \text{Mental Model} & \text{Main Safe Operation}\\
\hline
? extends T & \text{Producer} & \text{Read as }T\\
? super T   & \text{Consumer} & \text{Write }T
\end{array}
\]

\textbf{Do not memorize ``extends = read'' blindly.}

The real reasoning is:

\[
\boxed{
\texttt{extends}
\Rightarrow
\text{unknown subtype}
\Rightarrow
\text{safe to read as }T
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\texttt{super}
\Rightarrow
\text{unknown supertype}
\Rightarrow
\text{safe to write }T
}
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{Exceptions --- Java's Philosophy of Failure}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Thirty Eighth Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

\emph{Many beginners think:}
\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{Exceptions}
=
\textcolor{RosettaOrange}{Errors}.

{\ttfamily{\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{False}}}

An exception is not the failure.

An exception is information
about the failure.

Java is saying: 
Something unexpected happened.

What would you like to do?
\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The World Before Exceptions}

Consider C.

\begin{minted}{java} 
FILE* f = fopen("data.txt","r");
\end{minted}

\question{What if file does not exist}

Programmer manually checks:

\begin{minted}{java} 
if(f == NULL) {
    ...
}
\end{minted}

Everywhere.
Repeatedly.

Easy to forget.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Java's Approach}

Instead of:
Returning special values.
Java creates an object.

That object describes the problem.

That object is:

{\ttfamily{\textcolor{RosettaDeepGreen}{\textbf{Exception}}}}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Fundamental Event}

Imagine:

\begin{minted}{java} 
int x = 10 / 0;
\end{minted}

Java immediately says:
I cannot continue normally.

An exceptional situation
has occurred.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{What Actually Happens}

Java creates:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArithmeticException
\end{minted}

object.

Then:
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Throws} it.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Throwing}

Think:

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Return}
sends normal result.

\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{Throw}
sends failure information.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Mental Model}

Imagine:

\begin{verbatim}

Method A calls Method B calls Method C

\end{verbatim}

Problem occurs in C.

Exception travels \emph{upward}. 
Until someone handles it.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The Exception Highway}

\begin{verbatim}

main()

  |

  v

A()

  |

  v

B()

  |

  v

C()

  X

Exception

  ^
  |

travels upward

\end{verbatim}

This process is called: 
{\ttfamily{\textcolor{RosettaJade}{Stack Unwinding}}}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Handling Exceptions}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
try {
    int x = 10 / 0;
}
catch(ArithmeticException e) {
    System.out.println(
        "Cannot divide by zero"
    );
}
\end{minted}

Java says:

Try this code. If failure occurs,
jump to catch.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Meaning Of catch}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
catch(ArithmeticException e)
\end{minted}

e is just an object.

Like:
\begin{minted}{java} 
Student s;
\end{minted}
except the object describes
a failure.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Exception Hierarchy}

Everything derives from:

\begin{minted}{java} 
Throwable
\end{minted}

Tree:

\begin{verbatim}

    Throwable
     |
     +-- Error
     |
     +-- Exception
           |
           +-- RuntimeException
           |
           +-- IOException
           |
           +-- SQLException

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The First Deep Distinction}

Java separates:

Errors and Exceptions

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Errors}

Examples:

OutOfMemoryError

StackOverflowError

VirtualMachineError (Usually indicate JVM problems).

Not intended for normal recovery.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Exceptions}

Examples:

File missing.

Invalid input.

Database unavailable.

Network disconnected.

Programs often recover.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Checked vs Unchecked}

One of Java's most important ideas.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Checked Exceptions

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Unchecked Exceptions

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Unchecked Exceptions}

Derived from:

\begin{minted}{java} 
RuntimeException
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Examples:

\begin{minted}{java} 
NullPointerException
ArithmeticException
IndexOutOfBoundsException
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Compiler does not force handling.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Why?}

Because these usually indicate:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Programming mistakes.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Not environmental failures.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Example}

\begin{minted}{java} 
String s = null;
s.length();
\end{minted}

Produces:

\begin{minted}{java} 
NullPointerException
\end{minted}

Bug in program logic.

\section{Checked Exceptions}

Examples:

\begin{minted}{java} 
IOException
SQLException
ClassNotFoundException
\end{minted}

Compiler forces handling.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Example}

\begin{minted}{java} 
FileReader f = new FileReader("data.txt");
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Compiler says:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

You must handle possibility
that file does not exist.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Two Choices}

Option 1:

\begin{minted}{java} 
try-catch
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Option 2:

\begin{minted}{java} 
throws
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{throws}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
public void read()
throws IOException
{
    ...
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Meaning:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

I am not handling it.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

My caller must decide.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Important Insight}

throws

does NOT throw.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

throws

declares possibility.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

throw

actually creates and launches
an exception.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{throw}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
throw new IllegalArgumentException(
"Age cannot be negative"
);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Programmer manually creates
an exception.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{finally}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
try
{
    ...
}
finally
{
    ...
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

finally executes almost always.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Whether exception occurs or not.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Why finally Exists}

Suppose:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

File opened.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Exception occurs.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

File still must be closed.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

finally guarantees cleanup.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Exception Propagation}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
A()
calls
B()
calls
C()
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

C throws.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

If B ignores,

goes to A.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

If A ignores,

goes to main.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

If nobody handles:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Program terminates.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Custom Exceptions}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class InvalidAgeException
extends Exception
{
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Now domain language becomes
part of the program.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{OOP Interpretation}

Exception is simply:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

An object.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Polymorphism works.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Inheritance works.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Encapsulation works.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Everything learned earlier
reappears.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

Exception is an object describing failure.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

throw launches an exception.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

throws declares possibility.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

try contains risky code.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

catch handles failure.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

finally performs cleanup.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

Checked exceptions are enforced by compiler.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

Unchecked exceptions are usually programming mistakes.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Mental Compression}

Exception

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Failure Information

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

throw

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Launch Failure

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

throws

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Declare Failure

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

try

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Risky Region

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

catch

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Recovery

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

finally

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Cleanup

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Checked

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Compiler Enforced

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Unchecked

\[
\Rightarrow
\]

Programming Bug
% Future chapters

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
% PART V
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\part{Advanced Java}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{ArrayList --- The Dynamic Array}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\smallskip
\noindent
\hyperref[sec:quick_nav]{\emph{Back to Quick Navigation}}

\smallskip
\noindent
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{$\Rightarrow$}
\emph{Core \hyperref[chap:collections]{Collections Framework} class. Demonstrates \hyperref[chap:generics]{Generics} and \hyperref[chap:interfaces]{Interfaces} in practice.}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Twenty Fifth Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

Many beginners think:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

ArrayList

=

Linked List

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

False.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

ArrayList is fundamentally
an array.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

A very clever array.

\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Problem Revisited}

Suppose:

\begin{minted}{java} 
int[] arr =
new int[10];
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\textbf{\textbf{Question:}}

Can size become 20?

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

No.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Array size is fixed.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

This is the fundamental limitation.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Dream}

We want:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList<Integer>
numbers =
new ArrayList<>();
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Then:

\begin{minted}{java} 
numbers.add(1);
numbers.add(2);
numbers.add(3);
...
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

without worrying about size.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Hidden Truth}

Internally:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

ArrayList stores elements
inside an array.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Conceptually:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class ArrayList<E>
{
    E[] data;
    int size;
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Actual implementation is more complex.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

But this captures the essence.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Two Numbers}

ArrayList tracks:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Capacity

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

and

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Size

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Different concepts.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Example}

Suppose:

\begin{minted}{java} 
ArrayList<String>
names =
new ArrayList<>();
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Internally:

\begin{verbatim}

Capacity = 10

Size = 0

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

No elements yet.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{After Insertions}

\begin{minted}{java} 
names.add("Alice");
names.add("Bob");
names.add("Charlie");
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Now:

\begin{verbatim}

Capacity = 10

Size = 3

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Seven unused slots exist.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Critical Moment}

Suppose:

\begin{verbatim}

Capacity = 10

Size = 10

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Array is full.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\textbf{\textbf{Question:}}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

What happens during next add ()?

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Magic}

Java creates a larger array.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Copies elements.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Switches reference.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Old array discarded.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Visualization}

Before:

\begin{verbatim}

[1][2][3][4][5]

capacity = 5

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Need one more element.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Create:

\begin{verbatim}

[ ][ ][ ][ ][ ]
[ ][ ][ ][ ][ ]

capacity = 10

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Copy all data.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Then insert.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Important Observation}

Resizing is expensive.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Must copy every element.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Cost:

\[
O(n)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Then Why Is add() Fast?}

Excellent question.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Most insertions do NOT resize.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Example:

\begin{verbatim}

Capacity = 1000

Size = 217

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Adding one element merely places
it into the next slot.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Cost:

\[
O(1)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Amortized Analysis}

Occasionally:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Very expensive resize.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Most of the time:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Very cheap insertion.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Average cost:

\[
O(1)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

called

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Amortized O(1)

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Accessing Elements}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
numbers.get(500);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

How many operations?

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Array lookup.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Cost:

\[
O(1)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Very fast.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Why Fast?}

Arrays support:

\[
address
+
index
\times
size
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Direct computation.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

No traversal.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Insertion At End}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
numbers.add(42);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Usually:

\[
O(1)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Unless resizing occurs.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Insertion In Middle}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
numbers.add(0,99);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\textbf{\textbf{Question:}}

Where should existing elements go?

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Need shifting.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{verbatim}

Before

[1][2][3][4]

Insert at front

After

[99][1][2][3][4]

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Everything moves.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Cost:

\[
O(n)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Deletion}

Observe:

\begin{minted}{java} 
numbers.remove(0);
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Again:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

All remaining elements shift.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Cost:

\[
O(n)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Summary Table}

\begin{center}

\begin{tabular}{|c|c|}
\hline
Operation & Complexity \\
\hline

get(index)
&
O(1)
\\
\hline

set(index)
&
O(1)
\\
\hline

add(end)
&
Amortized O(1)
\\
\hline

insert(middle)
&
O(n)
\\
\hline

delete(middle)
&
O(n)
\\
\hline

search
&
O(n)
\\
\hline

\end{tabular}

\end{center}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Mental Model}

ArrayList optimizes:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Random Access

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Not

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Middle Insertions

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{When To Use ArrayList}

Use when:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Many reads.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Frequent indexing.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Appending at end.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Rare middle modifications.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Rosetta Rules}

\begin{rosettarule}

ArrayList is built on arrays.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

ArrayList has capacity
and size.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

ArrayList grows by resizing.

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

Random access is O(1).

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

Appending is amortized O(1).

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

Middle insertion is O(n).

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\begin{rosettarule}

Middle deletion is O(n).

\end{rosettarule}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Mental Compression}

ArrayList

\[
=
\]

Array

\[
+
\]

Automatic Resizing

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Strength:

\[
O(1)
\]

Random Access

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Weakness:

\[
O(n)
\]

Middle Modifications

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{LinkedList -- Escaping The Array}
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\smallskip
\noindent
\hyperref[sec:quick_nav]{\emph{Back to Quick Navigation}}

\smallskip
\noindent
\textcolor{RosettaRoyalPurple}{$\Rightarrow$}
\emph{Part of \hyperref[chap:collections]{Collections Framework}. Compare with ArrayList for performance and usage trade-offs.}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Twenty Sixth Wrong Idea}

\begin{trap}

Many beginners think:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

LinkedList

=

ArrayList

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

with different syntax.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

False.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Their internal structures are
completely different.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Nearly opposite design philosophies.

\end{trap}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Fundamental Array Problem}

Observe:

\begin{verbatim}

[10][20][30][40][50]

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Insert:

\[
25
\]

between:

\[
20
\]

and

\[
30
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Need:

\begin{verbatim}

[10][20][25][30][40][50]

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\textbf{\textbf{Question:}}

What happens to:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

30

40

50

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Must shift.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Cost:

\[
O(n)
\]

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{The Radical Idea}

Instead of storing data together:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Store pieces separately.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Connect them using references.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Node}

The fundamental building block.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Conceptually:

\begin{minted}{java} 
class Node
{
    int data;
    Node next;
}
\end{minted}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Contains:

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Data

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

and

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Reference to next node.

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

\section{Visualization}

Suppose:

\begin{verbatim}

10 -> 20 -> 30 -> 40

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Actual memory:

\begin{verbatim}

NodeA      NodeB      NodeC

10         20         30

  \          \          \
   \          \          \
    ---------->---------->

\end{verbatim}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%

Nodes need not be adjacent.
